### **Claude** · 2026-10-10 08:23

That's normal, and it isn't your fault. Instagram puts DMs from accounts people don't follow into the **Message Requests** folder, and most small businesses never open it. So they haven't said no. They just haven't seen your message yet.

**Here's what to do instead:**

1. **WhatsApp first.** Most of these accounts have a 05 or +966 number in their bio, and WhatsApp messages get seen. Send the message below.
2. **Comment on their latest post.** Comments show up in their notifications and they do read those.
3. **Make one real free sample.** I'd start with riyadh.detailingbeast_autocare. Once it's actually made, you can honestly say "I made you a free sample", and that gets far more replies.

**WhatsApp message (copy and paste):**

> السلام عليكم 👋 أنا من Pixel & Co.، نصمم مواقع للمشاريع الصغيرة في السعودية. شفت حسابكم في انستقرام وعجبني شغلكم. أقدر أسوي لكم نموذج موقع مجاني بدون أي التزام. يناسبكم؟
>
> Hi 👋 I'm from Pixel & Co. We build websites for small Saudi businesses. I saw your Instagram and loved your work. I can make you a free sample website, no commitment. Interested?

**Comment on a post (copy and paste):**

> شغل نظيف 🔥 أرسلت لكم رسالة بالخاص، شيكوا على طلبات الرسائل 🙏
> Great work 🔥 I sent you a DM, check your message requests 🙏

I've also updated your notes to say the DMs haven't been seen.

Want me to build the free sample for riyadh.detailingbeast now, or start the showreel site?

### **Claude** · 2026-10-10 09:10

Don't send them another Instagram DM. They're stuck in Message Requests, and a second DM would land there too. Reach the same accounts in a different way:

| Account | What to do |
|---|---|
| car_rental_in_riyadh | **WhatsApp first.** Rental businesses take bookings on WhatsApp, so they reply fast. |
| car_souq_riyadh | WhatsApp in English first (use the English-first message below). |
| _ericfades_ | WhatsApp his booking number in English. He does home service, so he checks it. |
| riyadh.detailingbeast_autocare | I build a free sample first, then you send it on WhatsApp. This is your best lead. |
| mr.steam001 | WhatsApp: "I can make your current website look more modern." |
| cardetails.sa | WhatsApp, plus a comment asking about their prices (real questions get answers). |
| diqqatriyadh26, barber_jeddah_feras | Comment on their newest post. Small accounts read every comment. |
| bearbematcha | Open their Linktree and use the WhatsApp or email there. |
| qamra_saa | Comment now. Message again when they relaunch. |
| power.teg | WhatsApp or email a partnership idea. Don't offer them a website. |

**English-first WhatsApp (for car_souq, _ericfades_):**

> Hi 👋 I'm from Pixel & Co. We build websites for small Saudi businesses. I saw your Instagram and loved your work. I can make you a free sample website, no commitment. Interested?
>
> السلام عليكم 👋 أنا من Pixel & Co.، نصمم مواقع للمشاريع الصغيرة في السعودية. شفت حسابكم وعجبني شغلكم. أقدر أسوي لكم نموذج موقع مجاني بدون أي التزام. يناسبكم؟

**Last try, 3 days after WhatsApp if there's still no reply:**

> مرحبا مرة ثانية 🙂 بس حبيت أتأكد وصلتكم رسالتي عن الموقع المجاني. إذا مو الوقت المناسب ما في مشكلة، بالتوفيق!
>
> Hi again 🙂 Just checking you got my message about the free sample website. If now isn't a good time, no problem at all. Best of luck!

**The rule:** after WhatsApp, a comment and the last-try message, if they still haven't replied, move on and keep finding new accounts (20 a day). Getting no reply from 8 or 9 out of 10 is normal.

### **Claude** · 2026-10-14 11:27

The Bear be matcha sample is now a proper ordering site 🐻🍵

**Link (same as before):** https://claude.ai/artifact/9pEEsRFekQjgoNVeX4HDxk

**What I improved:**
- **Customers can make each drink their way.** Tapping a drink opens a panel where they choose the size, the milk (oat, almond or coconut), how sweet, how much ice, and extras like cold foam or an extra matcha shot. The cup drawing changes as they choose, and the price updates.
- **It shows the shop exactly what they'd get on WhatsApp.** The order arrives as a tidy message with an order number, the customer's name, the pickup time, every drink with its options, and the total. This is the part that will sell it to them.
- **The checkout works properly.** Customers can change amounts in the cart, pick a pickup time that shows the real clock time, and the site asks for a name before sending. After sending, they see an "order ready" screen.
- **It looks more finished.** The bear blinks and sips, matcha leaves float, there are "Best seller" and "New" labels, the menu tabs follow you as you scroll, and the cart badge bounces when you add a drink.
- **It works on phone and computer,** in Arabic and English.

**What I sent you:** a 24-second phone video of someone ordering, plus 3 screenshots.

**Send to Bear be matcha (video first, it's the strongest):**

### **Claude** · 2026-10-14 11:32

Not yet. Wait for them to reply to the video first.

**Why wait:**
- They asked for one thing: an example. Sending a second, fancier offer before they answer can feel like pressure.
- Their problem is simple: taking orders without DMs. A 3D site doesn't fix that, it only makes the site look fancier, and it costs more time and money.
- If you show the basic sample and the 3D one together, they may not be sure which to choose, or they may think the basic one isn't good enough.

**When to bring it up:** once they say they like the sample, offer it as an extra:

```
وإذا حابين شي أفخم، نقدر نسوي نسخة متحركة فيها مؤثرات وأشكال 3D للماتشا 🍵✨ أرسل لكم مثال لو تبون.

And if you'd like something more premium, we can make an animated version with 3D matcha effects 🍵✨ Happy to send an example if you're interested.
```

**You don't need a special prompt.** I already know the style you like (the LUSTRE cinematic sample and the motion showreel). Just say "make the 3D version of Bear be matcha" when it's time.

**What I'll need from them to make it really theirs:**
1. Their real menu and prices
2. Photos of their drinks and shop
3. Their logo
4. Their WhatsApp number for orders
5. Branch location and opening hours

Remember: when they ask about price, check with your dad before you answer.

### **Claude** · 2026-10-14 15:21

Yes, keep going while you're motivated 💪 Just watch one thing:

**Stay under about 20 new DMs a day.** If Instagram sees lots of similar messages from a new account, it can block you from sending messages for a few days. You've sent about 8 new ones today, so you have room for about **12 more** on Instagram.

**WhatsApp doesn't count toward that limit.** If an account has a number in its bio, message it there. WhatsApp messages also get seen more often.

**To stay safe:**
- Change a few words in each message, like the business name and what they sell. Don't paste the exact same text every time.
- Leave 2–3 minutes between DMs.
- Follow and like their posts before you message.

**Where to look next:** the same places that worked today.
- The "Suggested for you" row on café and sweets profiles
- The accounts power.teg follows (that's where most of today's came from)
- Location tags for Riyadh and Jeddah cafés

### **Claude** · 2026-10-14 15:31

Good point. A business that already has a website can still be a client if their site is weak. mr.steam001 is already on your list for that reason.

**Treat them as a second priority, though.** It's harder to get a yes:
- They already paid someone for their site, so they'll want a good reason to change it.
- Many run their shop on Salla or Zid. Those handle payments and stock, so switching is a big change for them.
- Businesses with no website at all say yes more easily.

**When an upgrade pitch makes sense:**
- ✅ The site looks old, is slow, or breaks on a phone
- ✅ It's just a plain page with a logo and a phone number
- ❌ It's a working Salla or Zid store that looks fine. Skip those.

**How to check:** open their website link on your iPad, take 2–3 screenshots, and send them to me. I'll tell you if it's worth pitching and what we can do better.

**Upgrade message** (once we've checked their site):

```
السلام عليكم 🙂 أنا من Pixel & Co. شفت موقعكم، وعندي أفكار تخليه أسرع وأحلى على الجوال ويجيب لكم طلبات أكثر.
أقدر أسوي لكم نموذج مجاني لشكل جديد تشوفونه قبل أي قرار. يناسبكم؟
```

We should start posting no more tb h I Kathy find like real products take vids of them in real life and pics and make you help you make the best websites possible

### **Claude** · 2026-10-14 15:37

Yes, good idea 👍 When you DM a business, the first thing they do is open your profile. If they see real work there, they're much more likely to reply.

**One important rule:** don't make websites for real brands without their permission. That means no photos of another shop's products, logos or packaging on a sample we post. Your notes say this too, and it could get you in trouble.

**What you can film and photograph:**
- **Things without a brand on them:** a coffee you make at home, a plain cake, flowers, an unbranded perfume bottle, sneakers with the logo hidden.
- **Brands we invent**, like LUSTRE and MUNCH. You take the photos, I create the brand and build the site around them.
- **Real shops that give you permission.** Ask them: "Can I photograph your product for a free website sample?" That also works as a way to start a conversation with a new lead.
- **Bear be matcha's sample, but only if they say yes.** Ask them first.

**Post ideas for @pixelandco.design:**
1. **Screen recordings:** a smooth scroll through a site, like the matcha video (no real brand names).
2. **Before and after:** "A Linktree vs a real website."
3. **Site on a device:** film the site on your iPad on a café table.
4. **Process:** "From a photo of a cake to a full website in one day."

Aim for **3 posts a week**, and mark everything as a concept or sample.

**How to start:** take 5–10 good photos of one product (natural light, a plain background, a few angles) and send them to me. I'll create a brand name and a website for it, and record a phone video you can post.

### **Claude** · 2026-10-14 15:39

Yes, Scalp Elixir works for this, and it's better than a made-up brand because **it's your own store**. You don't need anyone's permission, and it's real work you can show clients.

**A few things to keep in mind:**
- **The Shopify store is already finished.** Changes to the store itself belong in your Scalp Elixir chat, so the two projects don't get mixed up. In this chat, we use it as a **portfolio piece** for Pixel & Co.
- **Keep the PURC label out of close-ups.** The bottle has another company's name printed on it. Turn the label away or film from the side, and let "Scalp Elixir" be the brand people see.
- **No "regrows hair" promises,** same as in your ads.
- **The store isn't open yet** (it's waiting on the Tap license), so don't send people to checkout until it launches.

I get it, it can feel like it takes away from the look. But there are good reasons to keep a label, and it doesn't have to be big:

### **Owner** · 2026-10-14 21:58

Wait, why do I need to add funds? Can't I just pay Apple Pay, bro?

### **Claude** · 2026-10-14 21:59

You **are** paying with Apple Pay. "Add funds" is just how Instagram does it on iPhone and iPad:

1. You pay with **Apple Pay / your Apple ID** (that's the "Pay and boost" button).
2. The money goes into your **Instagram ad balance** ("funds").
3. The boost spends from that balance.

So it's one payment, not two steps. The catch is that paying through Apple **always** adds the Apple fee. There's no way to avoid it inside the app.

**So, your options:**
- **Simple:** lower the budget to about **12 SAR/day for 4 days**, then tap Pay and boost with Apple Pay. Total is about **78 SAR**.
- **Cheaper, but more steps:** pay on the website (business.facebook.com), with no Apple fee.

Going simple is fine 👍 Just get your dad's OK first, since it's his money or card.

### **Owner** · 2026-10-14 22:07

I’m sorry but try this with the watch we made

BUILD AN INSANE LUXURY WATCH WEBSITE — CINEMATIC 3D EXPERIENCE

Act as a world-class luxury brand creative director, 3D product artist, motion designer, and senior frontend developer. Build an extraordinary, fully interactive website for the exact watch shown in my reference videos and images.

This must feel like an elite luxury watch brand with the cinematic quality of a premium product launch, the elegance of a high-end fashion campaign, and the technical sophistication of an interactive 3D showroom.

Do not build a generic ecommerce template. Build an unforgettable digital experience.

1. PRESERVE MY EXACT WATCH

The watch in my supplied reference material is the main subject of the entire website.

Study the reference images and videos carefully. Preserve the actual watch’s:

* Case shape, proportions, and thickness.
* Dial design, colors, markings, and hands.
* Bezel, crown, buttons, and other visible details.
* Bracelet or strap design, links, and materials.
* Original branding, logo, and distinctive features.

Do not replace my watch with a random luxury watch. Do not invent specifications, brand markings, materials, water resistance, or movement details that cannot be verified.

If the original reference videos are available, use them to understand the watch from different angles. If a real 3D model is needed, create or import an accurate model based on the references. If the reference material is insufficient to reproduce hidden details, keep those details conservative rather than inventing them.

2. CINEMATIC OPENING — MAKE THE FIRST 5 SECONDS INSANE

Start with a nearly black screen.

A narrow beam of light moves slowly across the metal surface of the watch. The camera reveals the bezel, the dial, the hands, and the reflections before revealing the entire watch floating in a dark studio.

The watch slowly rotates in 3D while dramatic lighting reveals its shape and finish. Fine particles float in the background, with subtle atmospheric fog and deep shadows.

Display a sophisticated headline beside the watch:

“TIME, REDEFINED.”

Add a short supporting line:
“Precision in every detail.”

Include two elegant buttons:

* EXPLORE THE WATCH
* DISCOVER THE DETAILS

Animate the headline with a refined staggered reveal. Bring the watch into focus through a cinematic camera movement. Add subtle parallax as the cursor moves.

Keep the introduction short, smooth, and premium. Do not make visitors sit through a long loading animation.

3. THE MAIN FEATURE — A REAL INTERACTIVE 3D WATCH

The watch must be the star of the experience.

Create a genuine real-time 3D viewer using Three.js or React Three Fiber.

Required interactions:

* Rotate the watch freely by dragging.
* Zoom in to inspect the dial, bezel, case, crown, and bracelet.
* View the front, back, side, and angled perspectives.
* Use smooth camera transitions between predefined angles.
* Add a subtle automatic rotation when the visitor is idle.
* Allow the user to pause the rotation and inspect the watch.
* Add an optional exploded-view mode if the model supports it.
* Provide a reset-view control.

Make the model feel physically present, with realistic metallic reflections, polished edges, believable shadows, and carefully controlled studio lighting.

If an accurate 3D model cannot be created from the supplied references, clearly structure the viewer so the correct model can be inserted later. Do not present a flat image as a functional 3D model.

4. INSANE SCROLL ANIMATIONS

Make the website feel like a cinematic journey through the watch.

As the visitor scrolls, pin the watch in the center of the screen while the camera and surrounding content transform around it.

Create a carefully choreographed sequence:

Scene 1 — The reveal

The watch rotates from an angled position into a dramatic front-facing view. The dial becomes sharply illuminated while the background remains dark.

Scene 2 — The details

Move the camera close to the dial. Reveal the hands, indices, bezel, and other visible details one at a time.

Scene 3 — The side profile

Rotate the watch to reveal its actual side profile, case thickness, and crown.

Scene 4 — The bracelet or strap

Move the camera along the bracelet or strap, highlighting its shape, links, texture, and finish.

Scene 5 — The full watch

Pull back into a complete hero shot. Let the watch rotate slowly while the headline and product description appear.

Scene 6 — The final reveal

Transition into a clean product-shopping section with a large product render, product information, and a clear purchase button.

Use GSAP and ScrollTrigger for precise scroll-linked animation timelines. Synchronize camera movement, object rotation, text reveals, and lighting changes with the scroll position.

Use smooth easing, layered depth, image masks, and seamless transitions. Avoid abrupt animation jumps and excessive motion.

5. LUXURY VISUAL DESIGN

Create a visual identity worthy of an expensive watch brand.

Art direction:

* Deep black and charcoal backgrounds.
* Silver and restrained metallic highlights.
* Sophisticated white typography.
* Elegant serif headlines combined with clean modern sans-serif text.
* Large amounts of negative space.
* Full-screen product imagery.
* Asymmetrical editorial layouts.
* Fine divider lines and understated navigation.
* Cinematic lighting and subtle reflections.

Avoid excessive neon colors, random gradients, generic rounded cards, cartoon graphics, and unnecessary glassmorphism.

The website should look expensive because of its composition, photography, typography, and detail—not because every element is glowing.

Use a temporary luxury brand identity only if the correct brand name and logo have not been provided. Make the identity easy to replace.

6. PREMIUM MICRO-INTERACTIONS

Make every interaction feel deliberate and tactile.

Implement:

* A refined custom cursor on desktop, where appropriate.
* Magnetic buttons with subtle movement.
* Buttons that respond smoothly to hover and press.
* Animated underlines and elegant navigation transitions.
* Product images that respond subtly to pointer movement.
* Text reveals as sections enter the viewport.
* Smooth image zooms and masked transitions.
* A sophisticated full-screen mobile navigation menu.
* Polished loading states and clear feedback for user actions.

Use motion sparingly enough that the website remains elegant and easy to navigate.

7. PRODUCT STORYTELLING

Build a premium editorial section that showcases the watch through large images and carefully timed animations.

Include sections for:

* Design and silhouette.
* Dial and visible details.
* Case and exterior finish.
* Bracelet or strap.
* Craftsmanship and finishing.
* Technical specifications.
* Product gallery.

Every section should use the actual watch as its visual reference.

Only include technical claims that are supported by the supplied information. If specifications are unknown, leave editable placeholders rather than inventing facts.

Use large product photography, close-up crops, cinematic transitions, and minimal text.

8. INTERACTIVE PRODUCT GALLERY

Create a beautiful gallery containing the available watch reference images and additional renders where appropriate.

Include:

* Front view.
* Three-quarter view.
* Side profile.
* Back view, if available.
* Dial close-up.
* Bracelet or strap close-up.

Let visitors switch views through smooth transitions. Support full-screen image viewing, swipe gestures on mobile, and subtle zoom interactions.

Do not invent images of hidden watch components and present them as accurate photographs of the original.

9. REAL ECOMMERCE FUNCTIONALITY

Build a polished shopping experience if this is intended to be a store.

Include:

* Product name and verified description.
* Price and currency based on the supplied information.
* Product availability.
* A functional add-to-cart button.
* A working cart with quantity controls.
* Size or strap options only if relevant.
* Checkout integration if the required payment provider and credentials are available.
* A responsive product details section.
* Clear shipping and returns information when supplied.

All buttons must work. Do not create fake checkout buttons, pretend a payment succeeded, or show invented stock levels.

If payment or backend integration is not configured, clearly identify what remains to be connected.

10. ADVANCED PAGE TRANSITIONS

Create a consistent motion system across the entire site.

Use cinematic zoom transitions, full-screen image reveals, sliding editorial panels, elegant fades, and carefully timed camera movements.

Navigation between pages should feel like entering the next scene of a film rather than abruptly loading another template.

Use GSAP for complex animation sequences and lightweight CSS transitions for simpler interactions. Use WebGL shaders only when they genuinely improve the experience.

11. MOBILE EXPERIENCE

Make the website exceptional on phones and tablets, not just desktop.

Adapt the 3D viewer to touch controls. Provide touch-friendly buttons, swipeable galleries, responsive typography, and simpler animation sequences where necessary.

Reduce expensive visual effects on lower-powered devices. Respect reduced-motion preferences and preserve a usable experience if WebGL is unavailable.

12. PERFORMANCE AND ENGINEERING

Build a real, maintainable website using the existing project’s technology stack wherever possible.

If starting from scratch, use React, Three.js or React Three Fiber, GSAP with ScrollTrigger, and optimized assets.

Requirements:

* Lazy-load heavy 3D assets.
* Optimize geometry, textures, and image sizes.
* Avoid unnecessary dependencies.
* Keep scrolling smooth and responsive.
* Prevent layout shifts.
* Provide sensible loading and error states.
* Ensure all navigation and interactive elements work.
* Test touch controls, resizing, and mobile layouts.
* Avoid memory leaks and unnecessary animation loops.

Do not sacrifice performance just to add more effects.

13. FINAL QUALITY CONTROL

Before finishing, inspect the entire website and test every interaction.

Verify:

* The watch resembles the actual supplied reference.
* The 3D model is genuinely interactive if described as such.
* The scroll animations are smooth and synchronized.
* The camera transitions do not jump.
* Navigation, gallery controls, and shopping features work.
* The layout looks polished on desktop and mobile.
* Product details are accurate and editable.
* The website loads quickly and remains usable.

Do not stop after generating a hero section or a static mockup. Complete the entire experience.

FINAL OBJECTIVE

I want someone to open this website and immediately think they are looking at a luxury watch brand with an enormous creative budget.

The watch should feel like a physical object suspended in a cinematic world. Every scroll, camera movement, reflection, transition, and interaction should feel intentional.

Make it immersive. Make it sophisticated. Make it technically impressive. Make it memorable.

Prioritize the exact watch, exceptional art direction, genuine 3D functionality, cinematic motion, and flawless execution over unnecessary visual effects.

That isn't a website. It's a **3D animation film,** made by a professional 3D artist (@dimensionally.3d) in software like **Blender** or Cinema 4D on a powerful computer:

4. The computer then renders it frame by frame, which can take **hours or even days.**

It's like making a mini movie. That's why big brands pay thousands for videos like this.

**The first video (the watch exploding apart as you scroll):**

### **Owner** · 2026-10-15 20:53

What are these apps called? The ones like where like I can like do 3D modeling software and whatever and like those things.

### **Claude** · 2026-10-15 20:53

There are a few kinds. These are the main names:

**Pro 3D software (like the gold watch video):**
- **Blender:** free, and what most 3D artists on TikTok use. ⚠️ It runs on a **computer** (Mac or Windows). An iPad version is being worked on, but it may not be ready yet.
- **Cinema 4D** and **KeyShot:** what agencies use for product videos. Paid, and computer only.

**3D apps that work on iPad ✅**
- **Spline:** works in your iPad browser at spline.design. You make simple 3D scenes and it can export them for websites. **The easiest place to start.**
- **Nomad Sculpt:** shape 3D objects with your finger or an Apple Pencil, like clay. Cheap, one-time payment.
- **Shapr3D:** for precise product shapes like watches and bottles. Has a free version.

**Scan real products into 3D (useful for clients):**
- **Polycam** and **Luma AI:** walk around a product with your phone or iPad and the app turns it into a 3D model. Works best on devices with LiDAR (Pro models).

**AI that makes 3D from a photo:**
- **Meshy** and **Tripo:** upload a product photo and get a 3D model. The quality varies, but it's fast.

**AI that makes product videos:**
- **Runway, Kling** and **Google Veo:** turn a photo into a short cinematic clip. Label it as a concept.

**Where I'd start on your iPad:**
1. **Polycam:** scan your watch and send me the file
2. **Spline:** play around and learn the basics of 3D
3. Later, when you have a computer: **Blender**

Prices and free plans change, so check in the App Store or on the website before paying, and ask your dad first if anything costs money 👍

### **Claude** · 2026-10-15 21:04

Here's each tool in simple words:

**🧊 Blender (free)**
- **What it is:** pro 3D software, a digital workshop where you build any object in 3D.
- **What you'd use it for:** making realistic products (watches, bottles, perfumes) with real-looking metal, gold and glass, then animating them: spinning, opening up, the camera flying around.
- **Why it matters for you:** this is how the gold watch and Jacob & Co–style visuals are made. You'd make the visuals, and I'd build the website around them.

**✨ Spline (free plan)**
- **What it is:** an easier, simpler 3D tool made for websites.
- **What you'd use it for:** cute or modern 3D scenes (floating shapes, a 3D logo, a product you can spin with the mouse) that go straight onto a website.
- **Why it matters:** much easier to learn than Blender, so it's good for starting out.

**🎨 Figma (free plan)**
- **What it is:** a design app for planning what a website looks like before it's built.
- **What you'd use it for:** sketching layouts, choosing colours and fonts, and arranging photos and text, like a drawing board for websites.
- **Why it matters:** you can show a client the design first, and once they say yes, I build it.

**🎬 DaVinci Resolve (free) or CapCut desktop**
- **What it is:** pro video editing software.
- **What you'd use it for:** editing your Instagram reels (website recordings, before/after, music, text, smooth cuts).
- **Why it matters:** better posts bring more DMs and more clients.

**🤖 Claude Code on the laptop**
- **What it is:** me, running on your own computer instead of in the cloud.
- **What you'd use it for:** I build the site in a folder on your MacBook, and you open it in your browser and watch it change live.
- **Why it matters:** faster testing, easier file sharing, and you see the site the way clients will.

**🌐 Vercel or Netlify (free plans)**
- **What it is:** a service that puts websites on the internet.
- **What you'd use it for:** giving a client a real link like **cafename.sa** instead of a private preview.
- **Why it matters:** this is the step that turns a sample into a real, paid website.

**Order to learn them:** CapCut desktop → Figma → Spline → Blender. Easiest first, hardest last 👍

Yes, exactly. Each one has a different **purpose** (or **role**). Like a team where everyone has their own job:

| Tool | Its job |
|---|---|
| **Blender** | Makes realistic 3D products and animations |
| **Spline** | Makes simple 3D scenes for websites |
| **Figma** | Designs what the website looks like |
| **DaVinci / CapCut** | Edits videos for Instagram |
| **Claude Code (me)** | Builds the website with code |
| **Vercel / Netlify** | Puts the website online with a real link |

**How they work together:**
Figma (plan it) → Blender or Spline (make the visuals) → me (build the site) → Vercel (put it online) → CapCut (make a reel to show it off) 🔥

### **Owner** · 2026-10-16 11:30

With Claude like pro max what can i possibly do be honest

### **Claude** · 2026-10-16 11:30

Here's the honest answer. **Max doesn't give me new skills.** I can't suddenly make Blender-quality 3D or edit videos. What it mostly gives you is **more usage:**

**What you'd actually get:**
- **Much higher limits.** You can work with me (and Claude Code) for much longer before hitting a limit, which helps on days like today when we build whole sites and videos.
- **Longer, heavier sessions.** Big builds, many revisions and recording videos without stopping halfway.
- **Earlier access to new features and models** when they come out.

**What it won't change:**
- ❌ I still can't make photo-real 3D renders. You still need AI images (Gemini or ChatGPT) or Blender.
- ❌ It won't find clients for you.
- ❌ The sites won't look better just because of the plan. The quality comes from good images and clear direction.

**My honest advice:**
- **Stay on your current plan for now.** You're not hitting my limits often. Your ChatGPT limit was the problem, not mine.
- **Upgrade only once you have paying clients** and you're building several sites a week. Then it pays for itself.
- Prices and what each plan includes change, so check **claude.ai → Settings → Upgrade** for the current details, and ask your dad before paying 👍

Spending money right now makes the most sense on getting your **first client**, not on tools.

### **Claude** · 2026-10-16 11:45

That's a strong idea 🔥 That video is pure **motion graphics**: moving text that types out word by word, green liquid blobs morphing, UI cards sliding in, smooth zooms. **After Effects is exactly the tool for that style.** If you're already good at editing, this could be your secret weapon.

**How it fits Pixel & Co.:**
- **Me:** I build the websites and record smooth screen videos of them, like the TORQ videos.
- **You (in After Effects):** turn those recordings into reels like this one, with kinetic text ("What if your café had a website?"), transitions, the site on a laptop mockup and sound design.
- **Result:** posts that look like a real agency made them. That's how you get DMs.

**Good to know:**
- **After Effects is paid** (an Adobe subscription, monthly). Check the current price, and ask your dad first. Adobe sometimes has student discounts.
- **Free alternatives** if you want to start without paying:
  - **DaVinci Resolve:** its "Fusion" page does motion graphics
  - **CapCut desktop:** keyframes, text animations, easy transitions
- **Learn on YouTube:** search *"After Effects kinetic typography tutorial"* and *"After Effects UI animation tutorial"*. That's exactly the style in your video.

**Bonus:** I can also make **motion-graphic intro clips in code** (moving text, glowing blobs, transitions) and export them as videos for you to drop into your edits. That was the "motion showreel" idea from the start of this chat.

Want me to make a Pixel & Co. intro clip in this green, liquid style that you can use in your reels? 💪

### **Owner** · 2026-10-16 11:47

You can make clips like that with code or is it better if I finit myself on after effdects

### **Claude** · 2026-10-16 11:47

Honestly, **both work, and they're good at different things:**

**What I can do well in code:**
- ✅ **Kinetic text** (words sliding, typing and scaling in), clean and smooth
- ✅ **Glowing blobs, gradients, particles, light beams**
- ✅ **UI animations:** cards sliding in, buttons pressed, a cursor moving, a website scrolling
- ✅ **Brand intros and outros** (the Pixel & Co. logo reveal, "DM us" end cards)
- ✅ **Exact, repeatable, fast:** I can make 5 versions in different colours or languages in minutes
- ✅ It's **free** and works now, even on your iPad

**What After Effects does better:**
- 🎬 **Mixing real footage:** your videos, the client's product videos, phone recordings
- 🎬 **Timing to music:** cutting on the beat, sound design, whooshes
- 🎬 **Fine hand-tweaking:** you can see it and adjust each frame in real time
- 🎬 **Effects like motion blur,** realistic camera shake and 3D layers, done faster by hand
- 🎬 **Creative, unique edits** that feel human, not template-y

**Best way: do both together.**
1. **I make the pieces:** website recordings, animated text, intro and outro, background blobs
2. **You put it all together** in After Effects or CapCut, with music, cuts and transitions

That's how real agencies work: one person makes the assets, another edits the final video.

**For now (before the MacBook):** I can make a Pixel & Co. intro clip in the green, liquid style, and you put it in CapCut on your iPad. Want me to?

### **Claude** · 2026-10-18 15:49

Not yet. Don't talk about payment until they've seen the sample and say they want it. Money goes through your dad.

**The order:**
1. **Tomorrow:** send the sample. No price.
2. **If they like it and ask "how much?":** reply with this, then ask your dad:
```
الحمدلله إنه عجبكم 🙏 أرتب لكم السعر والتفاصيل وأرجع لكم اليوم إن شاء الله
```
(It means: "So glad you liked it 🙏 I'll sort out the price and details and get back to you today, inshallah")

3. **With your dad, decide:**
   - **The price.** Your range is 1,000–2,500 SAR. The 3D/advanced version costs more.
   - **How they pay.** The usual way is a bank transfer to your dad's account.
   - **When they pay.** Common: 50% before you start, 50% when the site is finished.
   - **Domain and hosting.** About 50–150 SAR a year. Decide who pays it.
4. **Then** send them the price your dad approved.

Don't give a price before your dad says yes, even if they push. "I'll get back to you today" is a normal, professional answer 👍

### **Claude** · 2026-10-18 17:10

I get it, 2 followers does feel bad. But honestly, I'd skip buying them:

- **People can tell.** 500 followers with 2 likes on each post looks fake, and that's worse than looking small. Business owners check this.
- **Instagram punishes it.** Fake followers never watch or like your posts, so Instagram shows your posts to fewer real people. It can also delete them later, or flag your account.
- **It's not honest.** Your whole pitch is "real work, free sample, no pressure", so fake numbers go against that.
- It's also money, so it would need your dad's OK anyway.

**Free ways to get real followers fast:**
1. **Friends and family:** ask everyone you know to follow and like your posts. That could be 30–50 people in one day.
2. **Your other account:** post a story saying "I started a web design business, follow @pixelandco.design 🙏".
3. **Post 3 times a week.** You already have great videos: TORQ, Atelier II, Scalp Elixir, and soon Bear be matcha (once they say yes). Reels bring new people.
4. **Comment on local businesses' posts.** Real comments, not "nice 🔥". Their followers see your name.
5. **The boost plan:** about 78 SAR (12 a day for 4 days) once your dad approves. Those are real people who actually saw your work.

**The truth:** small businesses care much more about your **sample site** than your follower count. Bear be matcha replied to you at 2 followers 💪

### **Owner** · 2026-10-18 17:23

I added the how it works highlights 2 times and it's still not showing.

### **Claude** · 2026-10-18 17:23

That's usually one of these. Check them in order:

1. **Refresh:** close Instagram completely (swipe it away) and open it again. Highlights sometimes take a minute to show.
2. **Did it go into "Work" instead?** Open the Work highlight and check whether your How it works story is inside it. If you tapped the existing highlight instead of **+ New**, it gets added there.
3. **Story archive is off:** go to ☰ menu → **Archiving and downloading** → turn on **Save story to archive**. Without it, highlights don't save properly.
4. **Close Friends:** if the story was shared to **Close Friends** only (green ring), the highlight only shows to them. Post it again as a normal story.
5. **Make it from the archive:** ☰ menu → **Archive** → **Stories** → open the story → tap **Highlight** at the bottom → **New** → name it **How it works** → **Add**.

Option 5 almost always works. If it still doesn't show, send me a screenshot of your profile 👍

No problem, here it is simpler:

**1. A domain is like rent, not something you buy once.**
The domain (like `bearbematcha.com`) costs about 50 SAR **every year**. You're paying for the **first year** inside the 1,250. Next year it has to be paid again, or the website stops working. So tell them now:
```
الدومين مدفوع أول سنة، وبعدها تجديده تقريباً ٥٠ ريال بالسنة
```
(Meaning: "The domain is paid for the first year. After that, renewing it is about 50 riyals a year.")

**2. Not every domain costs 50.**
- `.com` (like `bearbematcha.com`) costs about **40–60 SAR**. ✅ Easy, so use this one.
- `.sa` (like `bearbematcha.sa`) costs **more** and needs official papers, like a commercial registration. ❌ Skip it.

So just promise a **.com**, and you're safe 👍

Good choice, it's your strongest one 🔥 Before you boost, one honest fix and a few settings.

**⚠️ Fix the caption first.** Pulse is a **concept** we made, not a real client. So "we made it for an energy drink brand" sounds like a real job, and your rule is to always label samples. Use this instead:
```
موقع بـ 3D يتحرك مع السكرول 🤯⚡
صممنا هذا الموقع كنموذج لعلامة مشروبات طاقة: المنتج يلف ويتحرك كل ما تنزل بالصفحة.

```
(Meaning: the same caption, but it says "We designed this site as a **sample** for an energy drink brand.")

**Before boosting:**
1. **Ask your dad.** It's money.
2. **Wait 24 hours** after posting. If it gets views on its own, boosting it works better.
3. **Music:** Promote only allows **Commercial Sounds**. If your sound isn't allowed, switch to one from that library.
4. **Pay on the TikTok website, not in the iPad app.** In the app you buy coins through Apple, which costs more, like with Instagram.

**Settings in Promote:**
- **Goal:** **Profile views** (or **Messages** if it's offered). You want business owners to visit and DM you, not just likes.
- **Audience:** Custom → **Saudi Arabia**, ages **25–54**, interests like **Business** and **Food & Beverage**.
- **Budget:** start small, around **20–30 SAR a day for 3 days**, then see the results.

### **Claude** · 2026-10-20 12:09

Yes, that's the right plan 💪 Three strong sites, then "before and after" posts. Two rules so it stays legit:

**1. Before and after posts**
- With a **real client** (like Bear be matcha once they agree), ask them: "Can I post the before and after?" Most say yes.
- With **samples** (Pulse, TORQ), label them as **samples or concepts** in the caption. Never present them as real clients.

**2. Pictures for the new samples**
Don't take photos from other shops' pages, because that's their work. Instead:
- **Easiest: I give you AI image prompts.** You paste them into ChatGPT or Gemini, like you did for TORQ, and send me the results. I'll write exact prompts for the abaya, flower and cake photos.
- **Real photos:** film or photograph real things at home (flowers, a cake, an abaya on a hanger).
- **360 videos:** put the product on a turntable or a plate and spin it slowly while filming with your phone, or I'll write AI video prompts.

**3. The "10 TikTok ideas" list**
It's just a list of **10 videos you can post**, each one explained:
- **what to show**, like "scroll through the Pulse site on your iPad"
- **the first line of the caption** to hook people
- **the hashtags**

So when you don't know what to post, you just pick the next idea.

When you're back from the gym, want me to start with the **AI image prompts for the abaya store**? 🔥

### **Owner** · 2026-10-23 10:27

How about the ai post maker  how does it work

### **Claude** · 2026-10-23 10:27

Here's how the **AI post maker** works, simply:

**For the shop owner (3 steps):**
1. **Upload a product photo**, like a matcha drink, an abaya or a cake.
2. **Pick the goal:** new product, offer, opening soon or Ramadan/National Day.
3. **Get, in seconds:**
   - **3 captions** in Saudi Arabic and English
   - **hashtags** for their city, like #الرياض #كافيهات_الرياض
   - a **ready story design**: their photo with text and the logo in their brand colours
   - **the best time to post**

**What makes it better than just using ChatGPT:**
- It **remembers the brand**: logo, colours, tone (cute like Bear be matcha, or classy like Little Henri). Every post matches.
- **Saudi dialect** that sounds natural, not robotic.
- **Ready designs**, not just text, so they post straight away.
- **A calendar** with Saudi occasions, like Founding Day, National Day, Ramadan and Eid.

**How it makes money:**
- **Free** for 5 posts a month, then around **39–59 SAR a month**.
- Each post costs you a little in AI fees, so price it above that.

**The cool part:** I can build a **working demo right now** that really writes the captions with AI, here on your iPad. You can show it to clients like Bear be matcha.

**Honest downsides:**
- Lots of AI caption apps exist, so the **Saudi dialect and ready designs** are what has to make it stand out.
- A full app with logins and payments needs more work, the MacBook, and your dad for the money side.

Want me to build the demo? 🔥

### **Owner** · 2026-10-23 10:28

Wait can i make my own ai is it possible

### **Claude** · 2026-10-23 10:28

Honest answer: **yes and no** 😄

**❌ Building an AI from scratch (like ChatGPT or Claude):** not realistic. It takes millions of dollars, huge computers and years of work by big teams.

**✅ Making "your own AI" the way almost every AI app does:**
You build **your own app** on top of an existing AI (like Claude or GPT), and give it:
- **its own name and personality**, like "Pixel AI"
- **your instructions**: always write in Saudi dialect, short captions, matching the shop's brand
- **your data**: each shop's logo, colours, menu and past posts
- **your design**: your app, your logo, your prices

To customers, it **is your AI**. That's how most AI startups work.

**What you need for that:**
- an **API key** from an AI company. You pay per use, usually a few halalas per caption. Ask your dad before buying, and **never paste the key in chat**.
- the app itself, which I can build with you

**Even better for now:** I can build the demo using **me** inside the page (the artifact can ask Claude directly), so there's **no API key and no cost** to test it.

Want me to build **"Pixel AI Post Maker"** like that? 🔥

### **Claude** · 2026-10-23 10:41

I sent you the showreel video and DECISIONES.md 🎬

**What you get:**
- **The MP4:** 1920×1080, 60 fps, exactly 15 seconds, with music.
- **DECISIONES.md, in Spanish:** what I decided, what I left out, 6 rounds of fixes, and the total time (about 7 minutes from start to finish).

**The 7 scenes, cutting every 2 seconds on the beat:**
1. **Easing:** a curve draws itself and a dot rides it.
2. **Kinetic type:** "MOTION" slams in letter by letter, then each letter splits in half.
3. **Systems:** a grid of 364 shapes ripples out and morphs from circle to square to plus.
4. **Fluid:** orange liquid blobs that show "FLUID" only inside the liquid.
5. **Depth:** a 3D wave of 256 blocks, drawn without any 3D software.
6. **Rhythm:** a 00→60 fps counter, "EVERY FRAME IS CODE." and an equaliser that moves with the drums.
7. **Identity:** a hyperspace burst, then the **Pixel & Co. logo** assembles itself.

**Music:** I composed and synthesised all of it in code (120 BPM, A minor), with no samples. It has a kick, claps, hats, bass, an arpeggio, a build-up before the logo and a big final hit. I can't listen to audio myself, so I checked the levels and the spectrum with tools instead. Listen and tell me if anything sounds off.

```
showreel كامل بالكود فقط 🤯 بدون أي صور أو فيديو أو موسيقى جاهزة، حتى الموسيقى مكتوبة بالكود ⚡
```

### **Claude** · 2026-10-23 10:42

Haha, thank you 😄 Honest answer: it's not a new power, it's the **type of video**.

**Why this worked so well:**
- **Abstract motion graphics are what code does best:** shapes, type, lines, colour and timing. Everything is maths, so I can make it perfect frame by frame.
- **Before, we were trying to make real-looking things,** like a real watch, a real bottle or real hair oil. Code can't fake real photos well, which is why we needed your AI images or recordings.
- **Your prompt was very clear:** exact length, size, fps, "only code" and "go all out". Clear rules lead to better results.
- I'd already used the same technique (frame-by-frame rendering, synced to the beat) for the Scalp Elixir and TORQ videos. This time there was nothing realistic to fake, so it could go all out.

**What DECISIONES.md is:** the prompt you pasted came from a **Spanish** TikTok challenge, and it asked for a file called "DECISIONES.md" **in Spanish** ("decisiones" means "decisions"). It's just a short report explaining:
- what I chose (colours, fonts, the 7 scenes, the music)
- what I left out and why
- the fixes I made along the way
- how long it took (about 7 minutes)

You don't need it for TikTok. It was only part of the challenge. Want me to make an **English** or **Arabic** version?

**Cool idea:** motion graphics like this work great **for clients too**, like a 15-second ad for Bear be matcha or SAMRA: their name, products and prices animated to music. That's another service you could sell 🔥

### **Claude** · 2026-11-02 05:22

This chat is meant for Pixel & Co., and since this looks like graded schoolwork, I'll show you the **quick method** so you can do it yourself in a minute 👇

**The trick: Pascal's triangle gives the numbers in front of each term**
- Power 4 → **1, 4, 6, 4, 1**
- Power 6 → **1, 6, 15, 20, 15, 6, 1**

**Rule for (a + b)ⁿ:** the power of **a goes down** (n → 0), the power of **b goes up** (0 → n), and you multiply each term by its Pascal number.

**Q1 worked out: (m + 1)⁴**
= 1·m⁴ + 4·m³·1 + 6·m²·1² + 4·m·1³ + 1·1⁴
= **m⁴ + 4m³ + 6m² + 4m + 1**

**Q2: (y − z)⁶**
Use 1, 6, 15, 20, 15, 6, 1. Because it's **minus**, the signs **alternate**: + − + − + − +
→ y⁶ − 6y⁵z + … (you finish it)

**Q3: (8h − 3j)⁴**
Use 1, 4, 6, 4, 1 with a = **8h** and b = **−3j**. Raise the **numbers too**: (8h)⁴ = 4096h⁴, (8h)³ = 512h³, and so on.
→ First term: 1 · 4096h⁴ · 1 = **4096h⁴**
→ Second: 4 · 512h³ · (−3j) = **−6144h³j**
Keep going the same way.

Send me what you get for Q2 and Q3 and I'll check them ✅

### **Claude** · 2026-11-02 05:24

Here you go:

**Q2: (y − z)⁶**
= **y⁶ − 6y⁵z + 15y⁴z² − 20y³z³ + 15y²z⁴ − 6yz⁵ + z⁶**

**Q3: (8h − 3j)⁴**
= **4096h⁴ − 6144h³j + 3456h²j² − 864hj³ + 81j⁴**

How Q3 works, so you can show working:
- 1 · (8h)⁴ = 4096h⁴
- 4 · (8h)³ · (−3j) = 4 · 512 · (−3) = −6144h³j
- 6 · (8h)² · (−3j)² = 6 · 64 · 9 = 3456h²j²
- 4 · (8h) · (−3j)³ = 4 · 8 · (−27) = −864hj³
- (−3j)⁴ = 81j⁴

### **Owner** · 2026-11-02 05:25

Give me the streps to

### **Claude** · 2026-11-02 05:25

Here are the steps for all three, ready to copy 👇

**Q1: (m + 1)⁴**
Step 1: The Pascal row for power 4 is 1, 4, 6, 4, 1. Here a = m and b = 1.
Step 2: Write the terms out.
= 1(m)⁴(1)⁰ + 4(m)³(1)¹ + 6(m)²(1)² + 4(m)¹(1)³ + 1(m)⁰(1)⁴
Step 3: Simplify.
= **m⁴ + 4m³ + 6m² + 4m + 1**

**Q2: (y − z)⁶**
Step 1: The Pascal row for power 6 is 1, 6, 15, 20, 15, 6, 1. Here a = y and b = −z.
Step 2: Write the terms out.
= 1(y)⁶(−z)⁰ + 6(y)⁵(−z)¹ + 15(y)⁴(−z)² + 20(y)³(−z)³ + 15(y)²(−z)⁴ + 6(y)¹(−z)⁵ + 1(y)⁰(−z)⁶
Step 3: The odd powers of (−z) are negative, so the signs alternate.
= **y⁶ − 6y⁵z + 15y⁴z² − 20y³z³ + 15y²z⁴ − 6yz⁵ + z⁶**

**Q3: (8h − 3j)⁴**
Step 1: The Pascal row for power 4 is 1, 4, 6, 4, 1. Here a = 8h and b = −3j.
Step 2: Write the terms out.
= 1(8h)⁴ + 4(8h)³(−3j) + 6(8h)²(−3j)² + 4(8h)(−3j)³ + 1(−3j)⁴
Step 3: Work out the powers.
- (8h)⁴ = 4096h⁴
- (8h)³ = 512h³
- (8h)² = 64h²
- (−3j)² = 9j²
- (−3j)³ = −27j³
- (−3j)⁴ = 81j⁴

Step 4: Multiply each term out.
= 4096h⁴ + 4(512h³)(−3j) + 6(64h²)(9j²) + 4(8h)(−27j³) + 81j⁴
Step 5: Final answer.
= **4096h⁴ − 6144h³j + 3456h²j² − 864hj³ + 81j⁴**

- After your first 1–2 paying clients, when you have real work to show.

- Ask your dad first, and pay on the website (business.facebook.com), not in the app, to avoid Apple's extra fees.
